# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
node scripts/affected.mjs --base origin/main --worktree --run # landing: fast/affected; prints every reason and retains a receipt
scripts/test.sh                      # complete battery: schedule/on demand; packed qualification: publication
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
# Linux qualification host: strace >=6.8 on PATH (audits every successful TCP listen).
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Ordinary landings use `node snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs --base origin/main --run`
from the repository root; add `--worktree` for staged, unstaged and untracked
changes. `--json` prints the plan. Every command has a timeout, failures are
collected, and receipts live in `target/affected/`. Cargo runs with
`CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`.
The selector reads Cargo's workspace graph, closes source changes over reverse
dependencies, narrows test-only changes to targets/files, and follows client
imports/process consumers. Unknown paths, locks/manifests, build scripts,
proc-macros and shared test support fail broad with a reason per file. The
complete battery belongs to the schedule; packed qualification belongs to
publication. An affected receipt does not qualify a release. See
[LLP 0189](../llp/0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md#snapback-4-affected-selection-2026-10-24).

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS="-DHAVE_FDATASYNC=1 -USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT" cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS="-DHAVE_FDATASYNC=1 -USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT"` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7; fdatasync per LLP 3000.009.001.000.002 "SQLite durable write cycle"). `snapback4/.cargo/config.toml` supplies the same default to Cargo runs started inside `snapback4/`; it does not override an inherited variable, and a Cargo run started from the repository root does not read it; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

37:## 2. Default initialization
52:## 3. Password online starters
73:## 4. Proof diagnostics and account plans
94:## 5. Evidence and reviews
137:## 6. Wave-30 remaining friction (DX14)
214:## 7. Wave-31 production proofs and inspection (DX15)
267:## 8. Wave-32 builder reading and waiting (TOK1)
317:## 9. Wave-33 day-two reading and reruns (DX16)

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/inspect_merged.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/inspect_merged.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..e1bf62bf13
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/inspect_merged.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,46 @@
+//! Operator snapshots preserve tagged values even when historical column types differ.
+// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — merge current and retained images once per id, never via the active SQL scalar type.
+use super::{Inspection, Result, store_error};
+use crate::{Value, schema::{Schema, materialize_optional_fields},inspection::{InspectionCatalog,internal}};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+impl Inspection {
+    pub fn merged_records(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Vec<Value>> {
+        if internal(name) {return Err(("E_SQL_INTERNAL","data reads application tables only".into()));}
+        let (_,encoded)=self.active_schema_record()?;
+        let active:Schema=serde_json::from_str(&encoded).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
+        active.refuse_private(name).map_err(|e|("E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN_PRIVATE",e.to_string()))?;
+        let schema=self.retained_schema(active.clone())?;
+        schema.refuse_private(name).map_err(|e|("E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN_PRIVATE",e.to_string()))?;
+        let definition=schema.table(name).ok_or_else(||("E_SCHEMA",format!("no table {name}")))?;
+        if definition.ephemeral.is_some() {return Err(("E_SCHEMA",format!("data {name}: ephemeral rows live only in serving memory; no disk snapshot")));}
+        let number:i64=self.conn.query_row("SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1",[name],|row|row.get(0)).map_err(store_error)?;
+        let mut merged=BTreeMap::<String,BTreeMap<String,Value>>::new();
+        // Active images win, including explicit null; retained cells fill only absent names.
+        let mut query=self.conn.prepare("SELECT id,data,0 AS rank FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data,1 FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data,2 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY rank,id").map_err(store_error)?;
+        let rows=query.query_map([number],|row|Ok((row.get::<_,String>(0)?,row.get::<_,String>(1)?,row.get::<_,i64>(2)?))).map_err(store_error)?;
+        for row in rows {
+            let (id,data,rank)=row.map_err(store_error)?;
+            let mut value:Value=serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
+            if rank==0 {if let Some(table)=active.table(name) {materialize_optional_fields(&mut value,table);}}
+            let Value::Row(fields)=value else {return Err(("E_SCHEMA","inspection row has no row image".into()));};
+            let target=merged.entry(id.clone()).or_default();
+            target.entry("id".into()).or_insert(Value::Id(id));
+            for (column,value) in fields.iter() {
+                if definition.columns.contains_key(column) {target.entry(column.clone()).or_insert_with(||value.clone());}
+            }
+        }
+        let mut query=self.conn.prepare("SELECT id,column_name,data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id,column_name").map_err(store_error)?;
+        let cells=query.query_map([number],|row|Ok((row.get::<_,String>(0)?,row.get::<_,String>(1)?,row.get::<_,String>(2)?))).map_err(store_error)?;
+        for cell in cells {
+            let (id,column,data)=cell.map_err(store_error)?;
+            // Cell-only residue cannot create a row that no retained image owns.
+            if let Some(target)=merged.get_mut(&id) {
+                if definition.columns.contains_key(&column) {
+                    let value:Value=serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e|("E_SCHEMA",e.to_string()))?;
+                    target.entry(column).or_insert(value);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        Ok(merged.into_values().map(|fields|Value::Row(fields.into())).collect())
+    }
+}

     pub fn build_retained_views(

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/data_all.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/data_all.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..9a3f895a68
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/data_all.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,22 @@
+//! Complete operator inspection over one read-only retained snapshot.
+// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — retained cells supplement active images once per id.
+use super::Options;
+use snapback4_core::store_port::AnyInspection;
+use serde_json::json;
+use std::path::Path;
+pub(super) fn run(path: &Path, table: &str, options: &Options) -> i32 {
+    let inspected=(|| {
+        let conn=AnyInspection::open(path)?;
+        conn.begin_snapshot()?;
+        conn.merged_records(table)
+    })();
+    match inspected {
+        Ok(rows) => {
+            let rows:Vec<_>=rows.iter().map(snapback4_core::wire::to_json).collect();
+            if options.json { println!("{}",json!({"rows":rows,"more":false,"limit":null,"inspection":"active and retained records, merged by id"})); }
+            else { for row in rows {println!("{row}");} }
+            0
+        }
+        Err((code,message)) => { eprintln!("{code}: {message}"); 1 }
+    }
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/frozen-run.mjs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/frozen-run.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..f221862d5c
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/frozen-run.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,30 @@
+// API signatures: snapback4 guide api proof | client | auth | generated
+// Frozen source uses its saved API. Inline users are adapted for legacy file readers.
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm, readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
+import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
+import { join, dirname, resolve } from "node:path";
+import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
+import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
+const cwd = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const pkg = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(cwd, "package.json"), "utf8"));
+if (!pkg.scripts?.["client:calls:frozen"]) throw Error("No day-one client:calls was present at capture; add a business client before claiming frozen-client coverage.");
+let temporary;
+const env = { ...process.env };
+try {
+  if (env.CALLS_USERS?.trimStart().startsWith("{")) {
+    const users = JSON.parse(env.CALLS_USERS);
+    if (!users || Array.isArray(users) || Object.values(users).some(batch => !Array.isArray(batch))) throw Error("CALLS_USERS must be a role-to-account-arrays object");
+    temporary = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "snapback4-v1-users-"));
+    env.CALLS_USERS = join(temporary, "users.json");
+    await writeFile(env.CALLS_USERS, JSON.stringify(users), {mode:0o600});
+  }
+  if (env.CALLS_USERS && !temporary) env.CALLS_USERS = resolve(process.env.INIT_CWD ?? process.cwd(), env.CALLS_USERS);
+  const child = spawn("npm", ["run", "--silent", "client:calls:frozen"], {cwd,env,stdio:"inherit"});
+  const signals = ["SIGTERM", "SIGINT"];
+  const forward = signal => { try { child.kill(signal); } catch {} };
+  const handlers = signals.map(signal => {const fn=()=>forward(signal);process.on(signal,fn);return fn;});
+  try {
+    const result = await new Promise((resolve,reject)=>{child.once("error",reject);child.once("exit",(code,signal)=>resolve({code,signal}));});
+    process.exitCode = result.code ?? 1;
+  } finally { signals.forEach((signal,i)=>process.off(signal,handlers[i])); }
+} finally { if (temporary) await rm(temporary,{recursive:true,force:true}); }
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/frozen.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/frozen.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..14dd0552e6
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/frozen.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,78 @@
+//! A day-one headless client must keep its own handles and module boundary.
+// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — capture before editing; never overwrite historical evidence.
+use std::{path::Path, io};
+use serde_json::json;
+
+pub(super) fn files(dir: &Path, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> io::Result<Vec<(&'static str, String)>> {
+    if dir.join("v1").symlink_metadata().is_ok() { return Ok(Vec::new()); }
+    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(sources);
+    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() { return Err(io::Error::other("cannot freeze an unchecked v1 client; fix current schema first")); }
+    let mut files = Vec::new();
+    fn push(files: &mut Vec<(&'static str,String)>, path: String, text: String) {
+        if let Some((_,body))=files.iter_mut().find(|(name,_)|*name==path) { *body=text; }
+        else { files.push((Box::leak(path.into_boxed_str()),text)); }
+    }
+    fn walk(root: &Path, relative: &Path, files: &mut Vec<(&'static str,String)>) -> io::Result<()> {
+        let path = root.join(relative);
+        if !path.exists() { return Ok(()); }
+        if path.symlink_metadata()?.file_type().is_symlink() { return Err(io::Error::other(format!("frozen client refuses symlink {}", path.display()))); }
+        if path.is_dir() {
+            if matches!(path.file_name().and_then(|n|n.to_str()),Some("node_modules"|"dist"|"target"|".git")) { return Ok(()); }
+            for entry in std::fs::read_dir(path)? { walk(root, &relative.join(entry?.file_name()),files)?; }
+        } else if matches!(path.extension().and_then(|s|s.to_str()), Some("mjs"|"cjs"|"js"|"ts"|"tsx"|"jsx")) || path.file_name().is_some_and(|n|n=="package.json") {
+            push(files,format!("v1/{}",relative.display()),std::fs::read_to_string(path)?);
+        }
+        Ok(())
+    }
+    // Save application modules, not node_modules, stores, credentials or build output.
+    for path in ["scripts", "src", "app"] { walk(dir,Path::new(path),&mut files)?; }
+    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(dir).into_iter().flatten() {
+        let entry=entry?;
+        if entry.path().is_file() && entry.path().extension().is_some_and(|e|matches!(e.to_str(),Some("mjs"|"js"|"ts"|"tsx"))) { walk(dir,Path::new(&entry.file_name()),&mut files)?; }
+    }
+    // Follow literal relative module imports beyond conventional source directories.
+    // Runtime-computed paths remain application responsibilities, as in Node itself.
+    let canonical_root=dir.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| std::env::current_dir().unwrap_or_default().join(dir));
+    let mut index=0;
+    while index<files.len() {
+        let (saved,text)=&files[index];
+        let source=dir.join(saved.strip_prefix("v1/").unwrap());
+        let mut imports=Vec::new();
+        let bytes=text.as_bytes();let mut cursor=0;
+        while cursor<bytes.len() {
+            let quote=bytes[cursor];
+            if !matches!(quote,b'\''|b'"') {cursor+=1;continue;}
+            let start=cursor+1;let mut end=start;
+            while end<bytes.len() && bytes[end]!=quote {if bytes[end]==b'\\' {end+=1;}end+=1;}
+            if end>=bytes.len() {break;}
+            let before=text[..cursor].trim_end();
+            let module=before.ends_with("from")||before.ends_with("import")||before.ends_with("import(")||before.ends_with("require(");
+            if module {if let Some(value)=text.get(start..end) {if value.starts_with("./")||value.starts_with("../") {imports.push(value.to_owned());}}}
+            cursor=end+1;
+        }
+        for import in imports {
+            let path=source.parent().unwrap().join(import);
+            if !path.exists() {continue;}
+            let canonical=path.canonicalize()?;
+            let relative=canonical.strip_prefix(&canonical_root).map_err(|_|io::Error::other("frozen relative import escapes the app; keep client modules inside the project"))?;
+            let saved=format!("v1/{}",relative.display());
+            if files.iter().any(|(name,_)|*name==saved) {continue;}
+            if path.symlink_metadata()?.file_type().is_symlink() {return Err(io::Error::other("frozen module imports must not use symlinks"));}
+            if path.is_file() {push(&mut files,saved,std::fs::read_to_string(path)?);}
+        }
+        index+=1;
+    }
+    for (name,text) in sources { push(&mut files,format!("v1/snapback/{name}"),text.clone()); }
+    push(&mut files,"v1/snapback/generated/api.ts".into(),snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound));
+    push(&mut files,"v1/snapback/generated/package.json".into(),"{\"type\":\"module\"}\n".into());
+    let package: serde_json::Value = std::fs::read(dir.join("package.json")).ok().and_then(|b|serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
+    let command=package["scripts"]["client:calls"].as_str();
+    let mut frozen=json!({"private":true,"type":"module","scripts":{"client:calls":"node .snapback4-frozen-run.mjs"}});
+    if package["scripts"].is_object() { frozen["scripts"]=package["scripts"].clone(); }
+    frozen["scripts"]["client:calls"]=json!("node .snapback4-frozen-run.mjs");
+    if let Some(command)=command { frozen["scripts"]["client:calls:frozen"]=json!(command); }
+    for group in ["dependencies","devDependencies"] { if package[group].is_object() { frozen[group]=package[group].clone(); } }
+    push(&mut files,"v1/package.json".into(),super::pretty(&frozen));
+    push(&mut files,"v1/.snapback4-frozen-run.mjs".into(),include_str!("frozen-run.mjs").into());
+    Ok(files)
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/previous/day2-proof-0.4.44.mjs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/previous/day2-proof-0.4.44.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..fece782136
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/previous/day2-proof-0.4.44.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,160 @@
+// API signatures: snapback4 guide api proof | client | auth | generated
+// npm run day2:proof
+// Saved sources belong outside snapback/, where every .q file compiles together.
+// Add your business assertions at each release transition.
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile, readdir, stat } from "node:fs/promises";
+import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
+import { join } from "node:path";
+import { deploy, rollback, startServe, testSource, cli, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
+const args = process.argv.slice(2);
+// npm appends caller arguments after the script's default pair.
+const supplied = args.length > 3 && args[0] === "versions/v1" && args[1] === "versions/v2" ? args.slice(2) : args;
+const [v1, v2, destination] = supplied;
+if ((supplied.length !== 2 && supplied.length !== 3) || !v1 || !v2) throw Error("usage: npm run day2:proof -- versions/v1 versions/v2 [./deploy]");
+async function snapshot(path) {
+  if (!(await stat(path)).isDirectory()) return [[path, await readFile(path, "utf8")]];
+  const files = await readdir(path);
+  return (await Promise.all(files.sort().map(name => snapshot(join(path, name))))).flat();
+}
+const before = await Promise.all([snapshot(v1), snapshot(v2)]);
+async function assertBusiness({ url, release }) {
+  // BUSINESS_ASSERTION_BEGIN: add authenticated business and refusal assertions.
+  // Return [{ id:"table/id", fields:["changedField"] }, ...] for writes by the frozen v1
+  // client at a rollback stage. Include derived fields expected to change on forward; other values stay checked.
+  // An intentional delete returns {id:"table/id", fields:[], deleted:true};
+  // the row must exist before this callback and remain absent after forward.
+  // The default below proves storage conservation only, not business policy.
+  return [];
+  // BUSINESS_ASSERTION_END
+}
+async function checked(args) {
+  const result = await cli(args); assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr + result.stdout);
+  return JSON.parse(result.stdout);
+}
+// A release's renames resolve simultaneously against its immutable predecessor.
+// Cache authored sources too: returning forward may register that same source anew.
+const identitiesByRelease = new Map(), identitiesBySource = new Map(), declarationsByRelease = new Map();
+let lastOrdinaryIdentity;
+function tableIdentities(status, declarations, sourceKey) {
+  if (identitiesByRelease.has(status.release)) return identitiesByRelease.get(status.release);
+  let identity;
+  if (status.live.exact === false) {
+    const target = identitiesByRelease.get(status.live.source);
+    const parent = identitiesByRelease.get(status.live.parent);
+    assert.ok(target && parent, "view must name previously measured target and parent releases");
+    const viewHeaders = new Map(declarations);
+    const parentHeaders = declarationsByRelease.get(status.live.parent);
+    const targetHeaders = declarationsByRelease.get(status.live.source);
+    const matches = (headers, name, from) => headers.has(name) && headers.get(name) === from;
+    // Complete-current views retain their parent's names, even when an old name
+    // was reused for a new table. Alpha-adapted selective views carry the
+    // predecessor headers instead; matching names alone cannot prove lineage.
+    const completeCurrent = [...parentHeaders].every(([name,from]) => matches(viewHeaders,name,from));
+    identity = new Map(declarations.map(([name,from]) => {
+      const useParent = matches(parentHeaders,name,from) && (completeCurrent || !matches(targetHeaders,name,from));
+      const origin = useParent ? parent.get(name) : matches(targetHeaders,name,from) ? target.get(name) : undefined;
+      assert.ok(origin, `${name}: generated view has no measured table lineage`);
+      return [name,origin];
+    }));
+  } else if (identitiesBySource.has(sourceKey)) identity = identitiesBySource.get(sourceKey);
+  else {
+    const predecessor = lastOrdinaryIdentity;
+    const moved = new Set(declarations.filter(([,from]) => from).map(([,from]) => from));
+    identity = new Map(declarations.map(([name,from]) => [name,
+      !predecessor ? name : from ? predecessor.get(from) ?? `${name}@${status.release}` :
+      !moved.has(name) && predecessor.has(name) ? predecessor.get(name) : `${name}@${status.release}`]));
+    identitiesBySource.set(sourceKey, identity);
+    lastOrdinaryIdentity = identity;
+  }
+  assert.equal(new Set(identity.values()).size, identity.size, "distinct retained tables must have distinct identities");
+  identitiesByRelease.set(status.release, identity);
+  declarationsByRelease.set(status.release, new Map(declarations));
+  return identity;
+}
+async function snapshotAll() {
+  const status = await checked(["releases", directory, "--json"]);
+  const source = join(directory, "releases", String(status.release), "snapback");
+  const files = (await readdir(source)).filter(file => file.endsWith(".q")).sort();
+  const texts = await Promise.all(files.map(async file => [file, await readFile(join(source, file), "utf8")]));
+  const declarations = texts.flatMap(([,text]) => [...text.matchAll(/^table (\w+)(?: renamed from (\w+))?:/gm)].map(match => [match[1], match[2]]));
+  const tableIdentity = tableIdentities(status, declarations, JSON.stringify(texts));
+  const catalog = await checked(["sql", ".tables", "--release", directory, "--json"]);
+  const ephemeral = new Set(catalog.tables.filter(table => table.kind === "ephemeral table").map(table => table.name));
+  const tables = declarations.map(([name]) => name).filter(name => !ephemeral.has(name));
+  const rows = new Map();
+  for (const table of tables) {
+    const count = await checked(["sql", `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM "${table}"`, "--release", directory, "--json"]);
+    const page = await checked(["data", table, "--release", directory, "--limit", String(count.rows[0][0] + 1), "--json"]);
+    assert.equal(page.more, false, `${table}: incomplete snapshot`);
+    for (const row of page.rows) rows.set(`${tableIdentity.get(table) ?? table}/${row.id}`, row);
+  }
+  return rows;
+}
+function compareMoves(before, after, { required = before.keys(), ignore = new Map(), values = false } = {}) {
+  for (const id of required) {
+    assert.ok(after.has(id), `${id}: missing persisted row`);
+    if (values) {
+      const prior = { ...before.get(id) }, next = { ...after.get(id) };
+      for (const field of ignore.get(id) ?? []) { delete prior[field]; delete next[field]; }
+      assert.deepEqual(next, prior, `${id}: changed during release cycle`);
+    }
+  }
+}
+// An explicit deployment is retained for inspection. Only our temporary
+// deployment is removed; existing deployment directories are refused.
+const temporary = destination === undefined;
+const directory = destination ?? await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "snapback4-day2-"));
+if (!temporary) {
+  try { await stat(directory); throw Error(`day2:proof destination already exists: ${directory}; choose a fresh deployment`); }
+  catch (error) { if (error.code !== "ENOENT") throw error; }
+}
+let serve;
+try {
+  await testSource(v1, { fixtureOnly: true });
+  await testSource(v2, { fixtureOnly: true });
+  await deploy(directory, v1);
+  serve = await startServe(directory);
+  await assertBusiness({ url: serve.url, release: "v1" });
+  const oldRows = await snapshotAll();
+  await deploy(directory, v2);
+  compareMoves(oldRows, await snapshotAll()); // measure adoption before a callback can repair it
+  await assertBusiness({ url: serve.url, release: "v2" });
+  let current = await snapshotAll();
+  compareMoves(oldRows, current); // compare ids, never array order
+  const deletedByV1 = new Set(), bornInV1 = new Set();
+  for (let cycle = 0; cycle < 2; cycle++) {
+    await rollback(directory, 1);
+    const beforeV1Writes = await snapshotAll();
+    for (const id of deletedByV1) assert.ok(!beforeV1Writes.has(id), `${id}: a deliberate v1 delete resurrected on rollback`);
+    compareMoves(current, beforeV1Writes, { required: [...oldRows.keys(), ...bornInV1].filter(id => !deletedByV1.has(id)) });
+    const writes = await assertBusiness({ url: serve.url, release: "rollback-v1" });
+    assert.ok(Array.isArray(writes) && writes.every(write => typeof write.id === "string" && Array.isArray(write.fields) && write.fields.every(field => typeof field === "string") && (write.deleted === undefined || typeof write.deleted === "boolean") && (!write.deleted || write.fields.length === 0)), "return explicit field changes or deletions from the v1 callback");
+    assert.equal(new Set(writes.map(write => write.id)).size, writes.length, "one change declaration per id");
+    const changedByV1 = new Map(writes.filter(write => !write.deleted).map(write => [write.id, write.fields]));
+    const older = await snapshotAll();
+    for (const id of deletedByV1) assert.ok(!older.has(id), `${id}: a previously deleted row reappeared during the v1 callback`);
+    for (const write of writes.filter(write => write.deleted)) {
+      assert.ok(beforeV1Writes.has(write.id), `${write.id}: a declared delete must start from a visible row`);
+      assert.ok(!older.has(write.id), `${write.id}: declared delete did not remove the row`);
+      deletedByV1.add(write.id);
+    }
+    for (const id of older.keys()) if (!current.has(id)) bornInV1.add(id);
+    compareMoves(current, older, { required: [...oldRows.keys(), ...bornInV1].filter(id => !deletedByV1.has(id)) }); // v2-native rows may lack a v1 view
+    await rollback(directory, "latest");
+    const beforeForwardCallback = await snapshotAll();
+    compareMoves(older, beforeForwardCallback); // includes newly inserted v1-client rows
+    compareMoves(current, beforeForwardCallback, { required: [...current.keys()].filter(id => !deletedByV1.has(id)), ignore: changedByV1, values: true });
+    for (const id of deletedByV1) assert.ok(!beforeForwardCallback.has(id), `${id}: a deliberate v1 delete resurrected on forward`);
+    await assertBusiness({ url: serve.url, release: "forward-v2" });
+    const forward = await snapshotAll();
+    compareMoves(current, forward, { required: [...current.keys()].filter(id => !deletedByV1.has(id)), ignore: changedByV1, values: true });
+    for (const id of deletedByV1) assert.ok(!forward.has(id), `${id}: a deliberate v1 delete resurrected`);
+    compareMoves(older, forward); // includes new v1-client rows
+    current = forward;
+  }
+  const history = await cli(["releases", directory, "--json"]);
+  assert.equal(history.code, 0, history.stderr);
+  assert.deepEqual(await Promise.all([snapshot(v1), snapshot(v2)]), before);
+  console.log("day2:proof passed: persisted ids and values across two rollback/forward cycles; sources unchanged (add business assertions for policy coverage)");
+} finally { await cleanup(() => serve?.stop(), () => temporary && rm(directory, { recursive: true, force: true })); }

 pub(super) fn apply(dir: &std::path::Path, additions: &[(&str, String)]) -> std::io::Result<()> {
+    let frozen:Vec<_>=additions.iter().filter(|(name,_)|name.starts_with("v1/")).collect();
+    if !frozen.is_empty() {
+        if dir.join("v1").symlink_metadata().is_ok() {return Err(std::io::Error::other("v1 already exists; no historical source replaced"));}
+        let nonce=std::time::SystemTime::now().duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH).unwrap_or_default().as_nanos();
+        let stage=dir.join(format!(".snapback4-frozen-{}-{nonce}",std::process::id()));
+        std::fs::create_dir(&stage)?;
+        let result=(|| -> std::io::Result<()> {
+            for (name,body) in &frozen {
+                let path=stage.join(name.strip_prefix("v1/").unwrap());
+                std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap())?;
+                std::fs::write(path,body)?;
+            }
+            if dir.join("v1").symlink_metadata().is_ok() {return Err(std::io::Error::other("v1 appeared during capture; no historical source replaced"));}
+            std::fs::rename(&stage,dir.join("v1"))
+        })();
+        if result.is_err() {let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&stage);}
+        result?;
+    }
     for (name, body) in additions {
+        if name.starts_with("v1/") {continue;}
         let path = dir.join(name);
         std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap())?;
         if *name == "package.json" || *name == "vite.config.mts" || *name == "scripts/frontend-proof.mjs" || *name == "scripts/day2-proof.mjs" { super::replace_file(&path, body)?; }

--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/day2.md
@@ -0,0 +1,128 @@
+# Day two: change, prove, release, return
+
+Start here with an existing app. Keep its store and saved sources. Before editing:
+
+```sh
+npx snapback4 init --adapt --day2 --apply
+npx snapback4 deploy ./deploy --save-version versions/v1
+```
+
+Adapt saves the current sources/fixtures in versions/v1 and versions/v2 if absent,
+and a frozen v1/ client with its own ESM package and generated API. Existing
+snapshots and application-owned proofs stay untouched. Run this before the v2
+edit; a snapshot taken afterwards is not historical evidence. If v1 is already
+saved/deployed, inspect `releases ./deploy --json`; reuse that saved source.
+
+Read only this card, ONE change route below, and `guide api proof` when writing
+proofs. Do not read deploy/production/evolution --full next: those commands list
+reference sections. Do not read dist/proof.js or the full declarations. Use the
+signature digest and a specific section only when a refusal needs it.
+
+## Choose one change route
+
+| Change | Read at most these sections |
+|---|---|
+| Rename a field/table/operation or argument | `guide rename --section opening`; `guide deploy --section 'Keep v1 clients working'` |
+| Loosen a required field, allow null or an empty album | `guide deploy --section 'Bridge a safety gate'`; `guide security --section 'N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING'` if the check asks for presence intent |
+| Add a required field computed from old values | `guide deploy --section 'Migrations and adoption'`; `guide deploy --section 'Which migration shape'` |
+| Change record kind, move media into children, split a list | `guide evolution --section 'Move media'`; `guide deploy --section 'Keep v1 clients working'` |
+
+For a spelling change use `renamed from`; retain old endpoint/argument aliases
+and result wrappers for v1 callers. Backfill computes historical values; it
+isn't maintenance on later edits. Guard every creating/updating path that can
+change a derived field. New required columns need a literal default or named
+backfill; `default now`/`viewer` cannot invent history. Media/reference moves
+need the supported extract shape, not a scalar transform. A loosened type does
+not authorize an absent value at every workflow transition: keep independent
+presence intent and current guards. Answer the actual check question; a
+presence tautology is no safety proof.
+
+## Preview, deploy v2, rollback and forward
+
+Update your working snapback/*.q and versions/v2 (source and contemporary
+fixtures); keep versions/v1 frozen. With production v1 already in ./deploy:
+
+```sh
+npx snapback4 check
+npx snapback4 test --source versions/v2
+npx snapback4 deploy ./deploy --schema versions/v2 --check
+npx snapback4 deploy ./deploy --schema versions/v2
+npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to 1 --check
+npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to 1
+npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to latest --check
+npx snapback4 rollback ./deploy --to latest
+```
+
+Replace 1 with the saved v1 release ID. Preview changes no live state and later
+writes can change its result. Await activation; no sleep is needed. Default
+rollback may use a registered compatible view retaining current guards. Inspect
+`releases ./deploy --json` for live source/view and row shape; a successful
+rollback alone does not establish frozen-v1 result compatibility. Use --exact
+only after deliberately reviewing the old source's broader policy. If preview
+requires a bridge or --restore-dormant, follow its concrete instruction and
+preview again. Never prune/reset/delete storage to make a proof pass.
+
+Run `npm run day2:proof` twice. The generated proof owns a fresh temporary store,
+checks persisted IDs/values across two rollback/forward cycles and cleans up.
+Its default business hook proves storage conservation only: add authenticated
+v1/v2 business calls, refusals and reads of v2-native rows. Return explicit
+changed-field/deletion declarations for intentional old-client writes. A custom
+proof must also preserve data and stop every host it starts.
+
+## Frozen v1 calls and repeatable users
+
+```sh
+BACKEND_URL=http://127.0.0.1:4400 CALLS_USERS='{"member":[{"email":"...","password":"..."}]}' npm --prefix v1 run client:calls
+```
+
+The frozen runner accepts inline JSON or a JSON file path, normalizes inline
+users to a private temporary file for inherited clients, and removes it after
+exit. It keeps the frozen generated handles. Run it on v1, live v2, rollback
+and forward; use BACKEND_URL from your own bounded serve proof. Missing day-one
+client:calls is explicit: author that business client before claiming coverage.
+The frozen script may intentionally fail a newly forbidden call; record the
+code separately from transport failures. Do not replace its API with v2's.
+
+```js
+import { uniqueName, freshUser } from "snapback4/proof";
+const title = uniqueName("proof");
+const member = await freshUser(host, {role:"member", provision:api.provision});
+// member has email/password/principal/token; freshUser uses real signup and
+// the ordinary operator provision mutation. Customize args for your schema.
+```
+
+Use freshUser once per role per proof run and reuse its session across that
+run's release cycles. uniqueName supplies fresh row names/intent IDs; it cannot
+clear a per-owner cap. A cap refusal on a second run needs a fresh owner/group
+or deliberate authorized cleanup. Never raise caps or delete the shared
+production store just to get green. Capacity refusals remain failures with a
+setup hint, never silently retried writes.
+
+## Production roster and complete inspection
+
+`guide api proof` includes startServe, provisionAccounts, account/roster shapes
+and exact options. For a persistent production driver use
+`guide production --section 'Serve script and reusable accounts'` only. Signup
+creates an account; an authorized provisioning mutation grants its role.
+
+```js
+import { startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
+const host = await startServe("./deploy", {
+  provisionUsers:{roles:{member:2, admin:2}, provision:api.provision}
+});
+const users = host.sessions; // {member:[account,account], admin:[account,account]}
+// Private harness readiness: {backendUrl:host.url, users}; do not log elsewhere.
+// Stop host in finally, or use your existing serve-check bounded driver.
+```
+
+Production provisioning reuses the named roster and logs in on rerun. Keep
+production/proof-accounts/default.json (0600) with its store; it holds credentials,
+stable provision IDs and partial completion. Use freshUser for isolated test
+batches, the persistent roster for production accounts. Neither bypasses rules.
+
+`data TABLE --release ./deploy --all --json` and
+`snapshotAll("TABLE", {release:"./deploy"})` include dormant cells and retained
+rows, merged once per ID. Active values win; deleted rows stay absent. This is
+administrative inspection, bypassing application read rules, not a client
+compatibility proof. Ephemeral rows exist only in serving memory. Inspect
+`releases ./deploy --json` separately for retained source history.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/pages.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/pages.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..55b3fcf39d
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/pages.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,59 @@
+//! Bounded reading sections; the compiled corpus retains complete examples.
+// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — explicit reference reads use the same byte budget.
+use super::{FULL_BYTES, reading_blocks};
+
+/// Flatten nested reference headings and split at complete paragraphs/fences.
+/// Long runnable references stay in the package, behind a file pointer.
+pub fn reader_card(name: &str, card: &str) -> String {
+    if card.len() <= FULL_BYTES { return card.into(); }
+    let title = card.lines().next().unwrap_or("# Guide");
+    let room = FULL_BYTES.saturating_sub(title.len() + 350);
+    let mut out = format!("{title}\n\nRead one section for your change. `guide {name} --full` lists sections; --repeat reprints a selection.\n\n");
+    let mut heading = "Opening".to_owned();
+    let mut page = String::new();
+    let mut part = 1;
+    let flush = |out: &mut String, page: &mut String, heading: &str, part: usize| {
+        if page.is_empty() { return; }
+        out.push_str(&format!("## {heading}{}\n\n{}\n", if part == 1 { String::new() } else { format!(" (continued {part})") }, page));
+        page.clear();
+    };
+    let mut fenced = false;
+    // Isolate headings even where source prose has no preceding blank line.
+    let mut prepared = String::new();
+    for line in card.lines().skip(1) {
+        if line.starts_with("```") {
+            if !fenced { prepared.push('\n'); }
+            fenced = !fenced;
+        }
+        if !fenced && (line.starts_with("## ") || line.starts_with("### ")) { prepared.push('\n'); }
+        prepared.push_str(line); prepared.push('\n');
+        if !fenced && (line.starts_with("## ") || line.starts_with("### ") || line.starts_with("```")) { prepared.push('\n'); }
+    }
+    for block in reading_blocks(&prepared) {
+        if block.starts_with("## ") || block.starts_with("### ") {
+            flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part);
+            heading = block.trim().trim_start_matches('#').trim().to_owned(); part = 1;
+            continue;
+        }
+        let block = if block.len() > room && block.starts_with("```") {
+            // This exact executable reference is packed by pack-recipes.mjs.
+            assert!(name == "testing" && block.contains("day2-accounts"), "oversized guide fence needs its own packed reference: {name}");
+            format!("Complete runnable reference: node_modules/snapback4/dist/recipes/day2-accounts.mjs. Run it with Node; read it only when adapting that account-cycle example.\n")
+        } else { block };
+        // Dense prose can exceed a section; split only prose at word boundaries.
+        let chunks = if block.len() > room {
+            let mut chunks = Vec::new(); let mut chunk = String::new();
+            for word in block.split_inclusive(char::is_whitespace) {
+                if chunk.len() + word.len() > room && !chunk.is_empty() { chunks.push(std::mem::take(&mut chunk)); }
+                chunk.push_str(word);
+            }
+            if !chunk.is_empty() { chunks.push(chunk); } chunks
+        } else { vec![block] };
+        for chunk in chunks {
+            if page.len() + chunk.len() + 1 > room { flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part); part += 1; }
+            page.push_str(&chunk); page.push('\n');
+        }
+    }
+    flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part);
+    out
+}

+  if (module === 'proof') title += 'Production: startServe(dir,{provisionUsers:{roles:{member:2,admin:2},provision:api.provision}}) returns host.sessions, shaped {member:[account,account],admin:[account,account]}. The private persistent roster is production/proof-accounts/default.json (0600): {version:1,plan,entries:[{role,index,email,password,id,done,principal?,args?}]}; keep it with its store. provisionAccounts reuses this roster; freshUser creates an isolated proof identity instead. Read guide production --section "Serve script and reusable accounts" for partial-setup recovery; never read dist/proof.js.\n';
   if (module === 'proof') title += 'Use probe:check for verification; probe:serve stays up until SIGTERM (deliberate harness run: --hold).\n';
   const render = entries => `${title}\n\`\`\`ts\n${entries.map(p => p.text).join('\n')}\n\`\`\`\n\nOther exports/options: dist/${module}.d.ts; follow its named imports only on demand.\n`;
   const selected = [];

@@ -247,3 +248,31 @@ export async function provisionAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorT
 export function devAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions> {

 }
+
+export interface FreshUserOptions {
+  /** Omit for a roleless password account. */
+  role?: string;
+  provision?: { readonly name: string };
+  service?: string;
+  args?: (account: StagingAccount) => Record<string, unknown>;
+  timeoutMs?: number;
+}
+/** Real signup and ordinary role provisioning, with fresh credentials on each call.
+ * Reuse the returned session within one proof's release cycles. No persistent roster.
+ * @throws the same auth/provision/environment refusals as account setup; partial accounts may exist. */
+export async function freshUser(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: FreshUserOptions = {}): Promise<StagingAccount> {
+  if (options.role !== undefined && !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/.test(options.role)) throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: freshUser role must name a role");
+  validatePlan({...options,...(options.role ? {roles:{[options.role]:1}} : {users:1})});
+  const response = await fetch(host.url + "/auth/config", {signal:AbortSignal.timeout(options.timeoutMs ?? 30_000)});
+  const config = await response.json() as {environment?: string};
+  const environment = config.environment;
+  if (!response.ok || !["development","staging","production"].includes(environment ?? "")) throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: freshUser requires a password proof host");
+  const sessions = await accounts(host, {
+    ...(options.role ? {roles:{[options.role]:1}} : {users:1}),
+    ...(options.provision ? {provision:options.provision} : {}),
+    ...(options.service ? {service:options.service} : {}),
+    ...(options.args ? {args:options.args} : {}),
+    ...(options.timeoutMs === undefined ? {} : {timeoutMs:options.timeoutMs}),
+  }, environment as "development" | "staging" | "production");
+  return sessions[options.role ?? "users"]![0]!;
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-fresh.ts b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-fresh.ts
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..58225c57b7
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-fresh.ts
@@ -0,0 +1,13 @@
+// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#9 — reruns isolate identities as well as row names.
+import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
+/** A unique row name or intent ID for this run; does not bypass per-owner caps. */
+export function uniqueName(prefix = "proof"): string {
+  if (!/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,31}$/.test(prefix)) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: uniqueName prefix must be 1..32 ASCII name characters, starting with a letter");
+  return `${prefix}-${randomUUID()}`;
+}
+/** Setup advice only; never retries a refused mutation or changes its code. */
+export function capacityAdvice(text: string): string {
+  return /holds at most \d+ rows/.test(text)
+    ? "Proof capacity reached: a repeat run shares rows with earlier runs. Use freshUser() for a fresh owner/group and uniqueName() for row names, or deliberate authorized cleanup. Do not reset the production store or raise its cap for the proof."
+    : "";
+}

     await bounded(run.closed, deadline(options.timeoutMs, 120_000), "proof cli close");
     if (run.error()) throw run.error();
-    return { code: run.code(), signal: run.signal(), stdout: run.stdout(), stderr: logText(run.logs.stderr), logs: run.logs };
+    const stdout=run.stdout(), stderr=logText(run.logs.stderr);
+    const advice=run.code() === 0 ? "" : capacityAdvice(stdout + stderr);
+    return { code: run.code(), signal: run.signal(), stdout, stderr: stderr + (advice ? "\n" + advice + "\n" : ""), logs: run.logs };
   } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
   finally { run.child.stdout?.destroy(); } // Secret capture must not pin the driver after a deadline.
 }

diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/publication-guide.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/publication-guide.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..9e9fe47a9a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/publication-guide.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,16 @@
+// Publication extractors deliberately consume every bounded reference section.
+// Builders should read one section at a time; this is a programmatic corpus check.
+import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
+export function guideReference(cli, card, options = {}) {
+  const invoke = args => {
+    const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [cli,"guide",card,...args], {encoding:"utf8",timeout:30_000,maxBuffer:4*1024*1024,...options});
+    if (result.status !== 0) throw Error(result.stderr + result.stdout);
+    return result.stdout;
+  };
+  const index = invoke(["--sections"]);
+  const numbers = [...index.matchAll(/^\s*(\d+)\. /gm)].map(match=>match[1]);
+  if (!numbers.length) return invoke(["--full","--repeat"]);
+  const pieces = index.includes("1. Opening (") ? [] : [invoke(["--section","opening","--repeat"])];
+  for (const number of numbers) pieces.push(invoke(["--section",number,"--repeat"]));
+  return pieces.join("\n\n");
+}
